# Codified lessons: the ledger (2026-10-19)

Why: Joe, 2026-10-19: "any durable learning that's not codified should be, now please." Tess took a week, much of it
rediscovering rules. Every durable lesson gets ONE row here: where it lives now and how hard it is held.

Status:
- ENFORCED: code refuses / fails a test (function cited; grepped on main; re-graded after the 2026-10-19 landing).
- ENFORCED-BRANCH: the guard exists and is tested, but only on an unmerged worker branch (named). Not yet on main.
- DOC: a written rule or a code default / warning only. An agent can still get it wrong.
- CHECK-PLANNED: should be a check; the one-line spec is the job (another agent is building these; the coordinator
  reconciles this column when they land).
- TASTE: Joe's call per character; stays a judgement (the recipe says where to ask).

Totals (2026-10-19, after landing the virtual dress form, tailoring rules and the MCP-enforced rows M1-M8 on main): 143 rows: CHECK-PLANNED 3, DOC 81, ENFORCED 55, TASTE 4. A row counts once, by its leading status.
New lesson = a new row the same day (check first, else a default, else a recipe / traps line). Recipe:
docs/notes/character_recipe.md. Traps (symptom -> cause): docs/notes/traps.md.
bin/ = the repo's bin/ (review, review-ab, approve, capped, slot, hooks; /mnt/data/hifipushie/bin links to it after bin/link-tools; row P14).

The pressed rule read flap <-> flap only, so the stand (between gorge and fall) got nothing: pressed_cells 0 on Garrett. A pair is pressed when one side is a folded piece's flap and the other a flap or an unfolded piece; two unfolded pieces never; the unfolded piece is the one laid, only where its nearest seam vertex is pressed (Cabrera & Flaherty: gorge seam pressed open) | fit round 5 (2026-10-19): collar end lay behind the lapel / under the front; the rule was never reached | pattern_form._press_flags / _pressed_pair (PRESS_UNFOLDED, default ON); tests/test_pattern_form.py test_unfolded_band_rides_with_the_flap_it_is_sewn_to (fails with it off) | ENFORCED (test). | C49 | A cached intermediate is keyed by the code that makes it (a hash of the modules' source + the overrides), never reused across a code change: the fit-round harness cached the shirt from round 4 before C46 landed, so rounds 4-5 fitted the jacket over a shirt whose collar fall stood 9-17 mm off its own stand (the code since lays it 3.4 mm off), read the jacket stand as INSIDE the shirt (-2.8 / -3.7 mm) and chased 'layer order' faults (454 jacket vertices in the shirt, the L gorge through its base) that the current code does not make (119 / 0). Before chasing a fault on a cached input, rebuild the input and re-measure. Layer order known answer: an outer two-piece collar over an inner on a neck cylinder lies outside it everywhere | fit round 6 (2026-10-19): the 'shirt collar outside the jacket stand' was a stale cache; flat neck clearance (+19.6 mm), the neck-point lift and the shirt's draft were fine | fit_round6/harness.py (inputs_<code hash>.pkl); tests/test_pattern_form.py test_nested_collars_keep_their_layer_order | ENFORCED (test) for the layer order; the cache rule is a harness convention (scratch), carried in the STATE page and here |
| C50 | An open collar (stand closure undone) is laid spread by ONE rule in every placement: the form applies the sim placement's cloth._spread_open_collar after the flaps turn and holds the swung ends (no gravity on the form, like worn-open fronts). Without it the form re-stood the neck pieces as a ring and the fall's turn folded the points to CF (Garrett shirt points 48 mm apart, concept 146). Bisect first: the jacket collar standing 18-28 mm off the neck at 60-120 deg was the SHIRT collar under it (jacket alone: 5 / 12 mm) | fit round 7 (2026-10-19): STAND trace per phase + NOUNDER A/B on the harness; shirt7.py trace (placement spread 150 -> band_init ring -> turn_flaps 46) | pattern_form.OPEN_SPREAD / open_collar_spread; tests/test_pattern_form.py test_open_collar_lies_spread_on_the_form (open 114 vs done up 44 mm, held 0.9 mm) | OPT-IN (KAT passes; on Garrett the jacket over the spread shirt got worse: stand 19 / 36 mm, through 119 -> 368: nothing presses the shirt collar under the jacket) |
| C51 | Two layers that touch: fit the OUTER garment first on the body plus the under layer's allowance (its least stand-off + one cloth), then the under garment with the outer as a CEILING where it covers it (covered = nearest point on the outer within one mesh edge sideways AND facing the way the body does there; contact the solve knows: weight LAYER_W, refactored when the contact set changes; the last round projects fully). A soft push lost to the cloth's lengths, a per-vertex hard projection tore the shirt (2000 edges past 30 %), and coverage without the facing test grabbed the shirt's side under the jacket sleeve. Alternating fits ("joint": outer over under, under under outer) INFLATE the outer (it is fitted over the unpressed inner first and its lengths give way: tube KAT outer +18 % stretch) | fit round 8 (2026-10-19): nested-tubes KAT both orders; Garrett harness8 base / outer_first / joint with OPEN_SPREAD | pattern_form.layered_fit, assemble(over=), covered(); tests/test_pattern_form.py test_nested_tubes_inner_pressed_under (outer_first: through 0, inner max 8.7 mm < outer 9.9) | OPT-IN: on Garrett outer_first halves the jacket neck gap (60 / 90 deg 18.4 / 28.0 -> 8.3 / 14.9 mm) but jacket-inside-shirt 119 -> 252 and the shirt's collar (an ARAP made piece) crumples into a ruffle under the jacket's gorge stack (1084 collar / stand edges past 30 %); joint fails its KAT |
| C52 | Big reads before details: DESCRIBE and check a garment's biggest reads first (the front's opening and closure state, silhouette, lengths), measured in the reference's own view, before any detail item (collar, notch, lapel width, pockets). Garrett's jacket spent eight fit rounds on the collar / notch while its front hung closed below the break (edge 18 mm off CF vs the concept's 74-90) and the lapels' V was 60 mm narrow: no item described the opening. | check: cloth_check.big_first (pattern gate rows ordered big to small; detail rows BLOCKED while a stage 1-2 row fails, unless waived); front_state / buttons_done restaged as stage-1 big reads; test_pattern_gate.py::test_big_reads_before_details. Doc: docs/state/fit_round9.md; front items in fit_round9/m9.py | fit round 9 2026-10-19 |
| C53 | An open front hangs from its seams: worn open, a front pivots at its break and its edge falls by its weight while the side seams, back and sleeves stay where the body holds them. On the gravity-free form, opening a front by its edge alone slides the whole isometric panel round the body (Garrett r9h: side seams 70 mm out at the hem, hem 574 vs 478, a peplum). Hold the open piece's seams to other pieces below the break at the CLOSED fit's positions and hang the free edge below the lowest worn-open point plumb (its drafted run-out kept); never on a piece its buttons hold. | check: pattern_form.open_seam_holds + worn_open_edge hang (OPEN_HOLD, OPEN_HANG); tests/test_pattern_form.py::test_open_front_hangs_without_moving_its_side_seams (open tube on a cylinder); m10 side-seam rows. Doc: docs/state/fit_round10.md | fit round 10 2026-10-19 |

Drafting the outer sleeve from the bare arm left Garrett's jacket sleeve (396 mm) barely wider than the shirt's (391): the shirt showed through both upper arms. Read the under layer's drafted width, not the body's biceps. | check: pattern_form.sleeve_room (+ sleeve_width); tests/test_pattern_form.py::test_sleeve_over_a_sleeve_has_room. Doc: docs/state/fit_round10.md | fit round 10 2026-10-19 |
| C55 | A silhouette fault is traced by ROW PROFILE against the reference and split form vs draft before a fix: the torso's outer width at every 25 mm row beside the concept's (one row hid that Garrett's whole upper torso is narrow, not just the waist), and the form's horizontal edge ratio at the row (1.00 = the form is faithful, the draft decides). A width read must be the OUTER torso edge, sleeves apart (m9's run-from-the-opening read 41 mm on open fronts). | check: fit_round10 silrows.py / waist.py / m10 (outer silhouette); docs/state/fit_round10.md Causes | fit round 10 2026-10-19 |

## MCP-enforced: the tools carry the rules (2026-10-19)

Joe: "we don't want to make it work just for this session, but anyone who uses the mcp." Each row is held by the MCP
server's own defaults, refusals and "next:" lines (server.py, next_steps.py), so a model with no brief follows it.
Audit: docs/notes/mcp_audit.md. Tests: tests/test_mcp_rules.py.

| # | Lesson | Codified at | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | A garment's order: pattern -> pattern check (flat) -> fit on the form -> judge clay + diag -> optional sim, a wrinkle source only -> game mesh. Post-sim closing / made pieces on the drape / hand finish are NOT steps (measured 2026-10-19, dress-garrett: strain p99 4.7 % -> 66-74 %) | server.INSTRUCTIONS; guide cloth (order, stages table, rule 3), guide_spec; next_steps after_* lines; test_instructions_and_guide_carry_the_order | ENFORCED (the text every client gets; supersedes C4) |
| M2 | No sim without a pattern-gate PASS, refused where the caller is: the MCP `dress` names pattern_check instead of a FAILED background job; `force` skips the stage checks only, never the gate | server.dress -> cloth_check.gate_status; next_steps.after_dress_refused; test_dress_refuses_without_a_pass_and_names_the_tool | ENFORCED |
| M3 | The pattern gate is a tool: per-item table FAIL / UNMEASURED first, flat sheet, diag of the placed pattern; a FAIL says "don't simulate" and lists the rows | server.pattern_check; test_pattern_check_fail_says_dont_simulate | ENFORCED |
| M4 | Fit on the form before any sim, and only on a pattern that passed flat | server.fit_garment (refuses without a PASS; pattern_form when present, the placed rest otherwise, said); test_fit_refuses_until_the_pattern_passes | ENFORCED (form itself: ENFORCED-BRANCH pattern-form-fit) |
| M5 | Garment looks show diag (panels + seams + turned; strain + layers) beside clay BY DEFAULT | server.DIAG_DEFAULT in look_cloth / pattern_check / fit_garment; test_garment_looks_default_to_diag, test_render_pattern_check_and_fit_default_to_diag | ENFORCED |
| M6 | Reviews take ONE view per call, no sheets / captions / claims, by an independent reviewer | server.review_view (one region by signature; reviewgate.refuse_inputs; bin/review, dry = a pair + prompt for a fresh agent); test_review_view_refuses_sheets_and_claims | ENFORCED |
| M7 | A pattern edit is drafted before it is saved and needs a new gate record; seams are walked after it | server.alter_pattern (true / match where the branch has them); test_alter_pattern_drafts_before_saving_and_needs_a_new_gate | ENFORCED |
| M8 | Stage tools end with the next step the gates require, generated from the result (not boilerplate); looks at a character say "reference beside it, judge before forwarding" | next_steps.py; look / look_cloth / pattern_check / fit_garment / alter_pattern / review_view / judge_asset; test_next_steps_follow_the_result | ENFORCED |
| M13 | A game garment (pattern-space re-grid of the drape) is never baked while an under layer crosses its cover, or a seam cracks, at ANY LOD; the refusal names the LOD, the pair, the count and the fix (finer edge, gap_rings, draft it further under; never by hand) | server.game_garment (export toolset) -> garment_game.build_lods (pierce_faces per LOD and under / cover pair, missing boundary edges), next_steps.after_game_garment; test_game_garment_refuses_a_crossing_lod_and_names_the_fix, test_game_garment_clean_writes_lods_and_says_judge | ENFORCED |

## Expressions, export, rig

| # | Lesson | Codified at | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | Expressions are mixes of expression parameters only; never sculpt per-character deltas | recipe "Expressions"; faceshapes.SHAPE_SCULPT (HP_SHAPE_SCULPT) still accepted | ENFORCED: faceshapes.refuse_sculpt (HP_SHAPE_SCULPT, face_fits.sculpt, sculpted deltas) unless HIFIPUSHIE_ALLOW_SHAPE_SCULPT=1 (WARNING) (tests/test_codified.py::test_sculpted_expression_deltas_are_refused) |

| C56 | A front edge is ONE line through the break: the lapel's outer edge runs on into the front edge below, no notch or step; the lapel only folds back along the roll line from the break. On the form, a worn-open front's edge targets carry the named points' corrections (linear in height between them, fading to none at the roll line's top), never "held where the swing put it" while one point is pinned elsewhere: the rigid swing meets only the last worn-open point, so the break's pin dragged that one vertex 7-9 mm out of the edge (Garrett r9h-r10i, Joe: "the notches cut in the bottom of the lapels"). Look at the FLAT outline first (clean here: step 0), then the fitted one. | check: pattern_form.edge_through_break + EDGE_FIELD (worn_open_edge); gate row front_edge_break (cloth_check.form_shape_rows, step <= 3 mm, turn <= 60 deg, flat + form); test_open_front_edge_has_no_step_at_the_break | fit round 11 2026-10-19 |
| C57 | A worn-open front hangs from its seams BELOW the break only; above it the lapel turns and the front follows. A hold's height comes from the NAMED worn-open points, never from derived pins (the edge pins run up the roll line to the neck): taken from them, the shoulder / neck seams were held closed under the turned lapel and the lapel top went through its base (Garrett 40-120 mm below the neck point L 129 / R 125 flap vertices; 45 / 45 unheld; Joe: "the tops of the lapels are embedded in the layer below"). | check: pattern_form.HOLD_NAMED; test_open_hold_stops_at_the_break_not_the_roll_top; measure flap_through_base + gate row lapel_on_base (share <= 5 %; test_front_crease_and_flap_through_measures_find_planted_faults) | fit round 11 2026-10-19 |
| C58 | An open front below the break hangs from the chest, not wrapped onto the waist: the form's seat attraction is off for a worn-open piece below its break (still pushed out of the body). Drawn onto the body while its edge is held open and its seams closed, the front had nowhere to put the opening but a fin at the front dart (Garrett: dart seam dihedral median 73 deg, r6 closed 8; Joe: "hopefully there's not a sharp fold here"). Creases are read with the mesh WELDED at its seams: a dart ridge is no interior edge. | check: pattern_form.HANG_FREE; front_creases + gate row front_crease (p90 / seam median <= 20 deg, sharp line <= 30 mm; KAT test_front_crease_and_flap_through_measures_find_planted_faults) | fit round 11 2026-10-19 |
| C59 | A worn-open front opens by SHEAR, not by sliding or stretching: with its chest and side seams held, an inextensible front cannot move its break off CF (the arc from break to side is shorter than the cloth by the opening). Before adding holds that keep the chest, check the cloth model can give the width; an isometric (shear-stiff) fit can't: held, it compresses (Garrett r12d stretch p99 74 %) and the lapels go through their base (tube 66/90 %). A swing START field changes nothing the relax undoes (chest drift 52 vs 62 mm). | tests/test_pattern_form.py::test_open_front_turns_its_lapel_not_its_chest (xfail strict: the woven-shear known answer); pattern_form ROLL_TURN / CHEST_HOLD comment | fit round 12 |
| C60 | A drafting rule written in tailoring_principles but not drafted is a fault, not a note: every R<n> that names numbers has an op that drafts them and a flat gate row validated on the reference draft (Jaeger) and on a planted fault. R25's cutaway sat undrafted for 12 rounds while a free round_corner [0.09, 0.06] stood in for it. | pattern_jacket.op_front_cutaway + cutaway_rows; tests/test_tailoring.py::test_cutaway_rows_pass_the_r25_draft_and_catch_a_square_corner, ::test_cutaway_reads_the_same_on_jaegers_front | fit round 13 |
| C61 | A measurement keeps the definition of the system that consumes it: FreeSewing drafts (Simon, Jaeger, our bodice) read shoulderSlope as hps -> shoulder point; our tape's line fit (10.9 deg on Garrett vs the chord's 22.5) put every shoulder point ~30 mm high and both shirt and jacket stood off the shoulder. Check a garment's shoulder line against the body's in the front view layer by layer (shoulderprof) before blaming the pad or the sleeve. | tailor.SHOULDER_SLOPE (opt-in "chord", comment); $FR13/shoulderprof.py | fit round 13 |
| C62 | Renaming / reshaping a pinned outline changes what the pins hold: a cut-away corner moved the worn-open cfHem pin from the corner's top to the curve near the hem and the waist / hem sweep rows fell (356 -> 335, 498 -> 443). After an outline op, re-check every worn_open / hold name it remapped. | op_round_corner shape: names on the cut-off part go to the nearest curve point (docstring); notes fit round 13 | fit round 13 |
| C63 | A fit energy is judged against the reference physics before it is aimed at a fault: woven shear (KES-F G/E 0.0055) let the seat's attraction wrap the form by shear (validation 2: bias shear p90 14.8 deg vs the sim's 0.7; waist / length / sleeve opening shrank, 12 -> 9 of 16), and did not open the lapel KAT, whose fault is weft crumpling across the roll line. Measure shear in the reference first; an energy that adds a freedom the reference does not use moves the fit away from it. | pattern_form.WOVEN opt-in (OFF); simshear.py ($FR14); validation 2 rows | fit round 14 |
| C64 | Strain on a shear-soft cloth is read along the grain, not on mesh edges: a 20 deg bias shear changes a diagonal edge ~17 % with warp and weft at length. Edge-strain gates (p99 < 5 %, the sleeve KAT's 6 %) misread a sheared fit as torn. | pattern_form info grain_stretch_pct + shear_deg (WOVEN); lapel KAT asserts grain_stretch | fit round 14 |
| C65 | A test number in a brief needs a source like any other: "a 20 deg shear < 5 % of a 2 % stretch" contradicts KES-F (1.67 x); kept as a strict xfail with the sourced value, the sourced known answer (energy ratio per unit strain = G/E) is the real test. | tests/test_pattern_form.py test_woven_shear_costs_its_sourced_share_of_a_stretch + xfail | fit round 14 |
| C66 | Closed != open above the top button when the open break moves off CF: the roll line pivots on the neck, so rows above the break shift by up to the break's swing (Garrett: 83 mm at the break, v_width 237 vs 149). Compare a closed fit to a closed target, or convert the open reference first. | docs/state/fit_round15.md NEXT 1 | fit round 15 |

| C84 | Judge a garment's ease over what is UNDER it, not only over the body: Garrett's jacket has the book's waist ease over the body (+110-119 mm, 14 %) but lies 1-11 mm over a shirt that keeps its full +109 mm as a stiff tube on the form, so it clings. An under layer's fullness under a jacket is soft (tucked, bloused), not a standing tube. | docs/notes/cloth.md "fit round 21 waist"; $FR21/waist.py | WRITTEN (gate row OPEN) |
| C85 | FIT ORDER: an under garment passes its OWN fit on the form (strain, and tucked: its waist fullness <= 10 mm off the body when worn under another) before any garment is fitted or simulated over it (Joe 2026-10-20: "We definitely shouldn't be fitting the jacket over a shirt that doesn't fit right yet"). fit_garment writes the fit record per garment fitted alone; fit_garment and dress refuse an outer garment until its under garment's record PASSes. | cloth_check.under_fit_status / fit_rows / write_fit_record, server.fit_garment + dress | ENFORCE (no KAT yet) |

Its front-view waist stayed 318-320 mm on every floor (concept 401): the pinch is the jacket's own shape (draft suppression + attraction into the waist hollow), not the shirt. Measure ease over the body AND the floor (C84) before blaming a layer. | docs/notes/cloth.md "fit round 22"; pattern_form.waist_layers; $FR22/gate84.py, widths.py | WRITTEN |

Garrett r23b: CB seat cup 80 -> 36 mm, but the seat swelled (width 413 -> 433) and the blind side A/B chose the old fit: kept opt-in. | pattern_form.BRIDGE / _Bridged / bridge_fraction; tests/test_pattern_form.py test_bridge_hourglass_tube

| C89 | A straight-cut ("falls straight down") jacket needs NO take-in anywhere: the waist was pinched by three things at once, the side panels' suppression, the CB + dart take-ins ("straight" kept them) AND the block's own fitted side seam (pattern_blocks fitted: up to 25 mm a quarter). R8 silhouette "plumb" takes nothing; its gate row waist_plumb checks the OUTCOME on the flat pattern (narrowest girth chest -> hips >= chest x 0.97, hips >= it) so a fitted block or a contour is caught, and front_dart must be absent. Garrett: min 890 -> 1143 flat; on the form with BRIDGE the front-view waist 398 (concept 401), seat 413 (413). | pattern_jacket.op_waist_suppression / tailor_rows (waist_plumb, front_dart), PLUMB_TOL; tests/test_tailoring.py test_plumb_silhouette_falls_straight_and_its_row_catches_a_pinch | ENFORCED-BRANCH (fit round 24) |

The jacket is no longer refused for a shirt waist nobody sees. | cloth_check.VISIBLE_SCOPED / standoff_row / visible_fit_rows / rejudge_under_visible, under_fit_status "provisional"; server.fit_garment; tests/test_cloth_layers.py test_under_fit_rows_judged_on_what_shows | ENFORCED-BRANCH (fit round 24) |

| C130 | WRAPPED FOLDS ARE DRAFTED BY DRAPING (Joe 2026-10-21, drape band round 1): collars, cuffs, waistbands, turtlenecks are described WORN (stand height, rise, where the fall lands on the layer below), built in 3D on the body + the garment's own cloth, and flattened (LSCM + ARAP, CB held straight) into the pieces; the flat piece is generated FROM the worn spec and the gate refuses a stale one. Geometry learned on the KATs: a fall hugging its stand down to the seam then lying on the shoulders is no flat piece (6-11 % strain): the fall is ruled from its roll to its landing line and the band above the stand is its mirror (a curved fold); the hairpin is a crease + (pi/2 - 1) w of turn; ARAP from a rectangle crawls on a sprung band (LSCM start). | C139 | THE BAND IS SEWN EDGE TO EDGE: ITS END CONTINUES THE FRONT'S EDGE (shirt spike 2026-10-21; Joe: "it feels like the pattern we started with should have known this?", and his open-shirt photos: the placket edge runs straight up into the band's end, the front edge rolls back SOFTLY with the band turning with it). Simon knows: its band's extensions past CF (9.3 mm button side, 14.8 mm buttonhole side) are its fronts' plackets (9 / 12-15 mm). Ours lost it three ways: (1) cloth_designs.json simon seam sews stand:leftBottomCf>..>rightBottomCf to the fronts' cfNeck>neck only: both extensions and placket tops loose; (2) cloth.band_ends cut the extensions to 7.5 / 9.0 mm and rounded them; (3) garment_kb.json no_tie open-neck rolls (neck -> button1 + 60, turned 170 = flat back) turned the neckline the band is sewn to: m2's band ends tipped ~45 deg out, the collar fronts ran in over them. e1 (seam leftBottomEdge>..>rightBottomEdge onto front.R:placketTopOut>cfNeck>neck / front.L:neck>cfNeck>placketTopFold1, band_ends off, no roll): band ends flush with the placket tops (0 mm), clean closed collar, mirror p95 1.3-1.7 mm. The open neck = fronts parting above the first fastened button with a SOFT roll that carries the band; not a 170 deg fold. LANDED 2026-10-21 (Joe: "update everything"): cloth_designs simon seam edge to edge + "band_ends": false (cloth.pieces reads it); garment_kb no_tie folds []; BUILD_VERSION 17. | C140 | A FOLD'S SENSE IS NOT A NEAR-TIE ON THE ANGLE (shirt spike 2026-10-21; Joe: "a recurring math problem where we don't realize the rotations are mirrored"): pattern_form._turn_flaps tried both senses and ranked by round(miss, 0.1 rad) FIRST, then by how far outside; the dihedral is unsigned, so both senses miss about equally and a mesh change (e0: 9 more seam vertices at the band ends) flipped the collar's fall INSIDE its stand (collar roll +82 vs -82, the diag's "other side" brown). Now: any sense within TURN_TOL (20 deg) of the asked turn qualifies, of those the further outside wins (FLAP_OUT_FIRST). e1 folds outside; n1 = n0 unchanged. | DOC (code default, worktree modest-napier; KAT owed: a collar whose two senses tie must fold outside) |
| C141 | A HINGE'S BENDING FRAME MUST BE A PROPER ROTATION (shirt spike 2026-10-21): flatarap's hinge springs rotated each rest vector by ONE face's 3x2 Procrustes frame (no det constraint) + that face's normal: stable for 10 iterations, then a face flipped, its normal flipped, the hinge pushed the corner through and it ran away (collar / stand p99 4.8 -> 59 % between 10 and 20 iterations, no handles). Each hinge now fits its own Kabsch rotation (det +1) over its 4 corners: 20 iterations hold (stand 4.4 %). Rest "flat" (pressed interfacing) or "made" (the closed fit's curvature: holds the fall closed, the points won't spread). Lives: spikes/shirt_spike/flatarap.py hinge_targets. | DOC (spike code) |
| C142 | HAND-TUNE BY PULLING UNTIL IT WOULD STRETCH (Joe 2026-10-21: "If I were hand-editing, I'd try to just pull the point out and to the side, and let it go as far as it could without stretching" ... "That kind of hand-tuning without stretching might be really important part of our process"): a pose is set by DRAGGING a handle along a direction in small steps, the cloth solved against its flat pattern each step, stopping at the last step whose stretch stays within a tolerance of the start's; never by pinning the handle at a guessed coordinate (pins dragged the collar's ends, band corners and the fronts' corners: front corner 22-152 %, collar crumple p95 0.34-0.72 mm) or by tuning stiffness (every bending variant roughened it). Shirt spike y3 (flatarap `pull`: points out + side + up, 3 mm steps, tol +1 %): points travelled 45 mm by themselves to 147 apart / 59-63 below HPS, V 63 below HPS (concept 147 / 50-61 / ~60), p99 4.8 % vs the pattern, collar crumple p95 0.28 mm. Pulls compose: a SEQUENCE (points to the limit, held; the inner corners apart a little; the points again) opened the throat further (c1 / c2: band ends upright, points 158 / 174 apart) at p99 5.0-5.6 %. A handle is an AREA, not a vertex (Joe: "we pinched and twisted the collar points"): a point dragged alone pinched and twisted its tip (c1 tip stretch 15-18 %, twist 60-80 deg); grabbed with a 15 mm patch (t15) the tip keeps the closed collar's 2-3 % / 15-28 deg. A RIGID patch can't turn while the cloth around it does: it folded the cloth over at its edge (t15: 18 / 28 faces at 12-15 mm, Joe: "totally broken"); the patch must TURN: drag the lead vertex alone, fit the patch's unstretched start shape onto that solution (Kabsch), pin, re-solve (u25, 25 mm: tips at the closed collar's crumple 0.06-0.10 mm). EVERY handle is an area, the band-end swing too (Joe's arrow at a lobe: the swing pinned 3 corner vertices per band end; stand TopCf 21 % -> 3 % with the band end within 15 mm turned as one: q40). Joe, same day: "Think of men pulling their shirts down along the G-line, popping cuffs, women pulling down skirts ... it's a flatten-this-along-interesting-geometry without stretching it much thing, and it's how humans put on clothes": handles grip points, borders (piece:a..b) or lists; turns (a band end swung, a cuff popped) come first and stay held. GENERAL TOOL 2026-10-21: src/hifipushie/garment_pull.py (pull / turn / smooth / style sequences; grips = 25 mm TURNING patches for points, a border moves as one; dir "easy"; reads crumple / tip_read / folded) + MCP style_garment. Found porting it: the stop is read against the LEAST stretch so far (a pull first takes up slack: the collar eased 4.41 -> 4.30 %), and few ARAP rounds per step are the regulariser (every solve converged crumpled the collar to p95 0.33 mm and stopped 16 mm short), with a converged CONFIRM before stopping (without it a slack strip stopped at 75 of its 100 mm). Lives: src/hifipushie/garment_pull.py; spikes/shirt_spike/flatarap.py (`pulls`, `patch`, `turn`, `swing_patch`, the origin), rough.py, tip.py, flips.py. | ENFORCED (tests/test_garment_pull.py: a slack strip pulled two ways stops within one step of its pattern length; two pulls in a sequence go as far as one; Simon's shirt on wf_simon v1, band ends turned 30 deg, collar points pulled out / side / up with turning patches, collar + stand smoothed: 142 apart, 64 / 60 below HPS, collar crumple p95 0.28 mm, tips as clean as the closed collar's (crumple, stretch max, <= 1 folded face); 'side' refused at CF). Open: with up 0.3 the right tip twists 63 deg (closed 35, spike sm1 47); the q40 recipe (up 0, hug, area turns) keeps it at 31 / 33 deg (asserted <= closed + 15) |
| C143 | PULL AND SMOOTH ARE THE TWO STYLING OPERATIONS (Joe 2026-10-21: "There's likely a 'smooth' operation that might be useful, in conjunction with the 'pull' operation ... those are the two main operations to styling the clothing one is wearing"). SMOOTH: in the brushed pieces alternate (a) fair: each vertex `lam` toward its one-ring centroid ALONG ITS NORMAL (a hand flattening a ripple), (b) restore: the flat-pattern solve with everything outside the brush and the pulled handles held; stop when stretch would exceed start + tol or roughness stops dropping (the pull's rule). sm1 (u25 + smooth collar + stand): roughness p95 0.67 -> 0.32 mm in the brush (collar 0.35 -> 0.28, stand 0.29 -> 0.16), stretch +0.3 pt. PULL DIRECTION findings: "outward along the collar's plane" (Joe) keeps the cloth clean but pulls the leaf taut (12 mm, then stretch: the leaf is held at its fold, it moves by swinging ACROSS its plane); "let the cloth choose" (w25: per step the least-stretch of ~9 directions round away-from-CF) went 81 mm with stretch FALLING, points 141 / 65-68 (2.5 min). Ported 2026-10-21: garment_pull.smooth (stretch read = the pull's, every piece with free cloth: on the brushed pieces alone smoothing the collar read +1.3 pt, the same move), dir "easy", style() sequences; MCP style_garment. Lives: src/hifipushie/garment_pull.py; spikes/shirt_spike/flatarap.py (`smooth`, `dir` plane / easy, the origin).  | ENFORCED (tests/test_garment_pull.py: a rippled sheet smooths (roughness halved) without its stretch rising; the shirt test smooths collar + stand to crumple p95 0.28 mm) |
| C144 | AN OPEN COLLAR HUGS THE NECK AT THE BACK AND SIDES; ONLY THE FRONT FLARES (Joe 2026-10-21, on b0: "reads wrong, like the collar is flared out into space all the way around ... it should mostly hug the neck on the back and sides. the flare is all toward the front"). Rule: when styling the front, HOLD the rest of the made piece where the relaxed closed fit has it (hug: band + collar within 110 deg of CB), from the first move; read it with csec.py (fall edge radius per angle vs the closed fit). h110: side 71 vs closed 68 (b0 82). Holding the UNRELAXED closed fit locked in its own 7 % strain: relax first, then hold. A HARD hold edge kinks the profile (q40: 29 deg at 79 deg from CB; Joe: 'one continuous swoop instead of three different angles'): FEATHER it, held to 70 deg then drawn back by a fading share to 130 (f2: 18 deg, side still 73 mm vs closed 68). | C147 | STYLING HOLDS WHAT IS DONE UP, AND READS ONLY THE CLOTH IT CAN MOVE (garrett collar round 1, 2026-10-22; probe s1 / s2): (a) worn_style held the fronts only below the top fastened button less 5 mm, so the button itself was free: Garrett's styled shirt pulled button 1 open 8.7 / 19.4 mm (first done button 203 below HPS vs the concept's 83). Now hold_mask("closure:<name>") also holds every FASTENED pair (<= 8 mm apart on the fit) with the cloth within 8 mm of both vertices on the pattern: gap 0.0 on Garrett and wf_simon. (b) the pull / smooth / relax stretch read counted every edge of a piece with free cloth, HELD edges included: Garrett's held fronts (tuck + side seams, 21-24 % p99 on the closed fit) pinned the read at 15.5 %, so the pull could never see the collar stretch and ran to max_move. The read counting only edges with a free vertex (edge_stretch free=) is used by the turn's tol stop ONLY: as the pull / smooth default it broke a known answer (test_shirt_collar_hugs_the_neck_at_the_back_and_sides: points 138 -> 121 apart) and changed nothing on Garrett, whose leaf is slack (the pull runs to max_move either way); reverted there (back to known good). (c) a TURN is a hand move too: "tol" stops it where it would stretch (Garrett's band ends at 65 deg took the free fronts' tops to p99 23 / 13 %, wf_simon's to 15 / 16 %). | ENFORCED (tests/test_garment_pull.py test_worn_style_holds_the_fastened_buttons: control (old mask) opens the top pair > 3 mm, the fix holds both pairs at 0 while the tops part) |
| C148 | A CRUMPLED OPEN COLLAR CAN BE THE CLOSED FALL'S SPRING, NOT THE STYLING (garrett collar round 1, 2026-10-22): Garrett's shirt collar (collarWidth 1.68, collarBend 0.015, set for fall cover in shirt rounds 2-8) ran its outer edge 74 mm over its sewn edge (wf_simon 45): on the CLOSED fit the fall already lay wavy (radius bumps at CB and +-75 deg, outer-edge turn 85 deg / 40 mm, a tip folded under), and every styling recipe inherited it (collar crumple .31-.37, tips .55-.75). Simon's default collarWidth / collarBend: spring 46, tips .09 / .12, styled crumple .23, 0 folded. Rule: before styling, read the closed collar's outer-edge turn and tips (open_collar_rows on the closed fit: tip crumple, folded faces, edge turn); a fault there is the pattern's, fix it before any style op. Also: a slack leaf moves without stretch (collar p99 flat to 60 mm), so "pull until it would stretch" alone can't place it (max_move decides). | DOC (gc_garrett collar_options_source; CHECK-PLANNED: fit_garment refuses to style a collar whose closed fit fails the tip / folded / edge rows) |
| C149 | EVERY VISIBLE PIECE IS ON THE FORM, WHATEVER A SIM HOLDS OUT (dress-tess s19, probe 2026-10-22; Joe: complete pattern, every piece): Tess's coat had construct sim_collar false (a made collar built on the drape after the sim, c14 / c15), and pattern_form.on_form drafts through the same cloth.build, so the form fitted a coat with NO collar and every collar rule (C137-C146) was unreachable. on_form now fits the whole draft (sim_collar false is a sim option). The coat's collar is drafted as the jacket's: collar_two_piece (stand + fall + fused undercollar), its roll folds declared. | pattern_form.on_form; tc_tess coat design (collar_two_piece) | ENFORCED (code) |
| C150 | A FLAT GIRTH READ AT A LEVEL COUNTS A VERTEX ON THE LEVEL (dress-tess s19): pattern_jacket._span_at used a strict sign change, and a contour op puts outline vertices exactly at the waist / hips levels: Tess's coat read chest / waist / hips 0 mm, so waist_suppression, hips_clearance (-36) and front rows judged nothing. Half-open crossings ((a > y) != (b > y)). Same family: the truing reader read an unfolded collar's CB (two mirrored seams meeting) as a seam end turning 178.5 deg, and the collar_fold_run reader took an unfolded stand's CB height (21 mm) for an end: both skip the CB of a piece cut on the fold now. A gate row that reads 0 or 180 is a measure to check before a fault to fix. | pattern_jacket._span_at; cloth_check.junctions; garment_reads (stand ends) | ENFORCED (code) |

Looks and reads judge the OUTER garment's own shape, rendered with the under one hidden (dress-tess s19 sheets: coat only); pierce counts stay as INFO. fit_garment's next step listed a pierce as "fix on the pattern ... before anything else": now an INFO line. | C154 | A LUMP UNDER THE FALL CAN BE THE SHOULDER SEAM BRIDGING THE NECK, IN THE CLOSED FIT (garrett collar round 2, 2026-10-22; Joe on gc1: "the only real issue is the lumpy part here", side view under the fall's outer edge): measured before fixing, it was the front \| yoke shoulder seam within ~50 mm of the neck point standing 28 mm off the body (a fin in a horizontal section, a pillow of front before it), the yoke sheared +7 / -5.5 %: Garrett's neck point (tape 'widest', 2 cm up the neck) sits on a near-vertical neck with the shoulder 40 mm below it 25 mm out, and the drafted seam bridges that corner. It was in the CLOSED fit (styling moved it < 1 mm); not the neck truing ops (nt1 / wt1); smooth / relax / pressing to the body on the styled fit did nothing or sharpened it (the shape is locked by the neckline and the cut, not slack); the line shoulder slope (ss11) left it (bump 12.5). wf_simon (sloping neck base): a smooth dome. Rule: read a lump by where it sits and when it appears (closed vs styled, section + strain) before choosing a lever; a styling op can't remove what the fit's pins and cut make. Measured from now on: garment_pull.neck_lump_rows (shoulder seam crest 5-35 mm from the neck point <= 32 deg, body cloth bump <= 8 mm; wf_simon 21 / 5.3, gc1 61 / 14.8), printed by fit_garment. | garment_pull.neck_lump_reads / neck_lump_rows; server fit_garment | ENFORCED (tests/test_garment_pull.py test_neck_lump_reads_tell_garretts_lump_from_a_smooth_shoulder: gc1 closed / styled FAIL both rows, wf_simon closed / styled PASS) |
| C155 | A SLOPING-SHOULDER BEND AT THE NECK END DOESN'T UNBRIDGE A HIGH NECK PIN (garrett collar round 2, coordinator's tailor test, 2026-10-22): Garrett's body falls 34 deg from the neck pin over the first 50 mm (the fitted seam 20.3). Bending the seam's first 50 mm 13.6 deg down on yoke + fronts (the "true" op at the neck point, before the neckline truing) left the lump as it was (crest 62, bump 15.0; gc1 61 / 14.8): a rotation keeps the seam's length, its dip is ~2-3 mm, and the seam between the two pins still bridges. It also turned the neck corner the collar is sewn to: points apart 154 -> 194, V 47 -> 72, crumple .23 -> .39. The whole-seam slope at the body's 33.9 deg can't be drafted: Simon at shoulderSlope >= 30 loses front.L's placketTopFold1 (cloth.spring_fall fails). Rule: a seam pinned at both ends bridges a hollow whatever its tangent; only more seam length or a lower pin lets it follow the body, and both move what is sewn to the neck. | (probes r2/sb34, sa34, ss30) | DOC |
| C156 | A ROLL'S ROWS ACROSS ARE SET BY ITS RADIUS, NOT BY MESH_REFINE; READ THEM BEFORE REFINING (garrett jacket fit round 27, 2026-10-22): folds.rows lays up to 5 rows over a roll's arc, spaced from its radius and turn (rows >= max(0.2 h, 2.5 mm) apart); mesh_refine only sets the size ALONG the rows and in the lattice beside them. Garrett's jacket lapel (roll_radius 6 mm in the round recipe, ROLL_R; the spec's 3 mm is overridden) already had 4 hinges 3.76 mm apart (pi r / 4 = 4.7: C137 met); the collar roll 2.52 mm. Grading the lapel / collar roll lines to 2.2 mm (along 3.64 -> 2.48 / 4.02 -> 1.77) made the closed fit WORSE: lapel.L turn 161 -> 79 deg (FAIL), lapel.R through its base 11 -> 19 %, collar twist 10 -> 33 deg/cm, front.L p99 12.6 -> 24.8 %, and it broke the placement (one side-panel start vertex left 6 cm out on the arm by _worn_levels' ramp: "the start is broken"; worked around round-locally, fit_round27/fix27.py). Rule: read the rows (fit_round27/rowmesh.py: spacing across and along) against pi r / 4 first; refine only a roll whose ACROSS spacing fails it. | (fit_round27 r27a0 vs r27a) | DOC |
| C157 | NO-SIM STYLING OPENS A JACKET CLEANLY BUT HAS NO LAYERS: KEEP THE RELEASE SIM FOR LAPPED / PATCHED PIECES (garrett jacket fit round 27, 2026-10-22; A/B vs round 26's one local release): garment_pull on the closed fit (top button released, back / sides / sleeves held so the side seams stay as fitted, each front edge pulled side + 0.25 out as one border, then the hem point) opened the fronts 87 mm with stretch p99 under 6 % and a straighter hem than the sim (cfHem 747 vs 736; no curled corners), but its flat-pattern ARAP has no contact: the lapels went through their own fronts (share 0.27 L, about 0.6 R vs the sim's 0.01 / 0.10), the pocket flaps sank 8 / 4 of 25 vertices (sim 0 / 1), and the fronts sank into the shirt. Also: a lapped closed front starts PAST CF (front.L edge x -25 mm): read a front edge SIGNED per side, never abs(x) (an abs read under-planned the first recipe by ~45 mm). Rule: styling is the method only once its solve carries a floor / layer order (push on the body and the under garment, a flap kept on its base); until then the sim opens a jacket. | garment_pull (wanted: layer floor) | SUPERSEDED 2026-10-23 by C170 / C174 (the styling carries the layer floor; the process has no release sim: muslin fit -> styling as worn, character_recipe Stage 5) |
| C158 | CULLING HIDES ONLY WHAT NO JUDGED VIEW SEES (garrett jacket fit round 28, 2026-10-22; Joe on r27sc: "It's almost good. I don't know why it thrashed the shirt though, that shouldn't even be possible if we're following our own rules"): the shirt under the jacket was gc1 to 0.0 mm; the CULL broke the approved layer's look. cloth_layers.hidden judged cover by DISTANCE (an outer point within 60 mm, < 12 mm to the side, the under cloth up to 15 mm OUTSIDE it: `pierce`, + margins at the outer's FREE edges): the jacket collar's fall is a turned edge (no free border, no margin), so the shirt collar lying over it in plain view counted as "poking through" and was cut, leaving ragged fragments (r27sc: stand 1468 / 5228, collar 4177 / 9995 triangles cut; rendered with CULL=0 the same geometry was clean). Also: round 27's BELT stand-in cut every shirt triangle below the belt line, the cuffs hanging by the hips too (223 / 223). Rule: an under triangle is hidden only if the outer garment or the body lies in front of it in EVERY judged view (front, 3/4 L / R at 37 and 45 deg, side L / R, back, each +-10 deg az / el: 72 z-buffers), the seen set grown 2 rings, a made piece with any seen triangle kept whole, hidden holes < 200 triangles kept, seen islands < 200 grown (never removed). Distance / pierce stays only as rule="margin" for a CLOSED fit worn open (no view of it shows the V). r27sc: 21968 -> 14244 hidden of 40342 (stand / collar / yoke / cuffs 0 hidden); shirt pokes through the jacket's shoulders now draw (seen cloth; C153). Belt now torso pieces only. | cloth_layers.hidden (rule "visibility" default), judged_dirs, _zbuffer (vectorised occluded) | ENFORCED (tests/test_cloth_layers.py test_cull_keeps_a_seen_collar_whole_and_culls_a_covered_back, test_cull_never_hides_cloth_a_judged_view_sees, test_zbuffer_matches_the_per_face_loop) |
| C159 | AN OPENED LAPPED FRONT CARRIES A GEOMETRIC WIDTH EXCESS: MEASURE THE CHORD BEFORE PICKING A RELEASE LEVER (garrett jacket fit round 28, 2026-10-22; Joe on r27sc: "the front of the jacket is crumpled, near the belly"): read first, then levers. The closed fit has none (band flap top -> break, offset from a 10-pass smoothed surface, p95 3.5 / 3.9 mm); the release makes it (14.5 / 12.8, tilt 40 / 34 deg): one ridge ~12 mm parallel to the edge, 20-60 mm in, 50-57 mm off the shirt (the shirt under it no rougher than elsewhere: not the placket). Opened, the edge travels from the lap (x -25..+6) to the concept's 63-91 mm off CF while the side seam is held (C96): the chord edge -> side seam drops 210-220 -> 135-155 mm, so 60-80 mm of front per side must fold; it hinges where the canvas halo round the break ends. Pin-only levers moved it a little or broke the rows: edge pinned at its ends (edges fell in 42 / 50 at 450, hem lift 30), pull 10 (edges in), side seams held above the waist only (no gain), a facing band to the hem (no gain); the plumb hang with side holds kept the rows and took it to 12.4 / 11.4 (kept). Rule: read crumple on the closed fit vs the release, and the chord the opened piece must span vs its cloth width, before choosing a release lever; an excess that size is a pattern question (lap / front width below the break for an always-open front), not a pin. | (fit_round28 crumple28.py / excess28.py / where28.py / ontop28.py) | DOC |
| C160 | THE NECK POINT IS WHERE THE NECK MEETS THE SHOULDER, AND MOVING IT MOVES ONLY THE NECK (garrett collar round 3, 2026-10-22; Joe: fix the lump by correcting where the shirt's HPS sits, measure right the first time): the tape's hps ("widest": the loop 2 cm up the neck) sits ON wf_simon's shoulder crest but 19 mm ABOVE Garrett's (a near-vertical neck on a flat trapezius shelf), and the seam pinned there bridged the corner (C154). FreeSewing's hps is "where your neck meets your shoulder": tailor "corner" = the crest's top just outside the neck (2.5 mm past the loop's widest x: a plane AT that x is tangent to a round neck and reads its wall), never above the loop: Garrett 23 mm lower / 24 back, Simon 5 lower. A draft hangs its lines from the hps (Brian: chest, armhole, waist as depths below it), and tapes taken FROM the new point changed by other amounts than its drop (Garrett back 515 -> 481, bust 249 -> 268 for 19 mm: the loop point's tapes start with a trip over the shoulder's top) and length_level's nape length is fixed from the hps: so the corner's hps tapes are the loop point's less the drop, length_level less "hpsDrop": every line keeps its body level, only the neck moves. Plumbed per draft (measure_defs CONSUMERS "hps" -> hps_definition -> cloth.model_body -> Body -> tailor.measure). Result on Garrett: ridge 61 -> 5-13 deg PASS, throat in gc1's frame kept (button 87.5 / 85.9, V 45 / 47, points 76 / 73-75), wf_simon better (21 -> 16 deg, 5.3 -> 4.5 mm); NOT passed: a front pillow beside the lowered point (bump 9.4-10.8 mm vs <= 8: the band holds the throat, so the front neck drafted from the neck girth is ~2 cm deeper than the body's hps -> throat drop; -15 mm of front neck depth took it to 8.4 but lifted the throat 11 mm), the styled fall buckles where it crosses the shoulder seam, the collar 11-15 mm lower round the back. PARKED (HPS_CORNER False; garment key hps "corner" opts in); gc1 stays. | tailor._neck_corner / measure(hps=), measure_defs.hps_definition / HPS_CORNER, cloth.length_level, garment_kb definition_holds | ENFORCED as opt-in (tests/test_measure_defs.py test_hps_corner_is_where_the_neck_meets_the_shoulder, test_hps_definition_is_the_consumers_unless_held) |
| C161 | A FIT THAT LETS CLOTH STRETCH HIDES MISFIT; READ THE FIT AS A MUSLIN (Joe 2026-10-22: "I'm wondering if we're hurting ourselves by allowing any cloth to stretch, any of the time"): the form fit's isometric edges (shear-stiff) lose to the neck pin (200 x an edge) and seams (400 x), so a pattern that can't reach shows as stretch (gc1's yoke corner p95 27 %, approved) and shear-stiffness invents red where the bias would have fitted (Garrett's shoulder point 37.5 % of faces > 3 %, Simon's 49.5 %). Refitted with warp / weft held and shear free (pattern_form.WOVEN, neck pin 2, WOVEN_SLACK OFF: on, the body attraction shrink-wraps all ease), the artefacts go (6.3 / 9.6 %) and what is left is real: Garrett's neck corner (threads p95 8 %, shear 47 deg past locking) and his hips (side seams 4-19 mm open). Rule: judge fit by THREAD strain by region on a woven fit (misfit read), never by a global strain p99 on the isometric one; surplus must stand off, not squash. | probe $G/r3 cfit_stiff.py / misfit.py / mapr.py; pattern_form.WOVEN (opt-in), NECK_PIN_W | SUPERSEDED by C162 (the muslin fit IS the fit, ENFORCED); the misfit read is the thread strain by region (C166, C169) |
| C163 | PROPOSED: A JACKET COLLAR'S HEIGHT AT CB IS SET BY THE SHIRT COLLAR UNDER IT (garrett jacket fit round 29, 2026-10-22): R16's roll line 25-30 mm (Mueller & Sohn) put the jacket collar's top 47 mm above HPS at CB over gc1, whose collar top is 52 (36 mm above its seam): the shirt showed 5 / 7 / 11 mm at CB / 45 deg / side (tell 10-20). The two neck seams sit level (15 / 16 mm above HPS): the neckline was not the fault, the collar's height was. Roll 20 mm (stand 16, fall 34: R16's relations kept): 14 / 14 / 14 on the closed fit; roll 16: the CB top stayed 38 (the fall wraps the shirt collar's top) and the side fall stopped covering the stand: worse. Rule (proposed): the jacket's roll line at CB = the under collar's height above its seam - the show (10-20 mm), held to R16's range only when the shirt collar allows it; waived by name on Garrett's gate (waive29.json) until tailor_rows can read the under garment's collar. | fit_round29 neck29.py; waive29.json (collar_stand, collar_stand_cb) | PROPOSED |
| C164 | A RELEASE JOB CARRIES ITS OWN PATTERN'S GATE KEYS (garrett jacket fit round 29, 2026-10-22): rounds 25-28's job builder (g25.py) wrote round 19's pattern_gate.json keys (job_r19a) into every release job, whatever pattern it was built from, so run_zozo checked an older pattern's PASS record: the pattern gate was bypassed by a stale key, not by an override. cloth_release.job records res["gate_keys"] in the cfg (dropped before writing) and cloth_release.write refuses other keys (GateKeyMismatch). Round 29 gated its own pattern (abfa0f45, PASS_WAIVED) before its sims. | cloth_release.job / write (GateKeyMismatch) | ENFORCED (tests/test_pattern_gate.py test_release_job_refuses_another_patterns_gate_keys) |
| C162 | THE MUSLIN FIT IS THE FIT, NOT A SIDE GATE: DIRECTION, NOT YET DEFAULT (Joe 2026-10-22: "shouldn't this always be the measuring process, always?"; "make the muslin fit the default fit ... report what breaks"): a separate check beside a flattering fit is how gc1 was approved with its yoke corner stretched 27 %. The form fit should hold warp / weft (WOVEN), pin the neck softly (as the shoulder) and never squash threads, so a misfit shows in the one fit everyone looks at; garments approved on the old fit are held on it by name until refitted and judged beside their approved look (garment_kb fit_holds; garment key fit_model). Tried as the default (commit 5aed0492 on branch claude/cool-chaum-8e917e, NOT on main): held gc1 bit-identical, wf_simon = the muslin probe, but 11 garment tests fail, by cause in cloth.md "the muslin fit as the default" (a)-(e); a free sheet shearing with nothing driving it is a real defect. Coordinator kept the flip off main (land only on exit 0).  LANDED as the default on claude/muslin-default (2026-10-22): no holds (C165), the reds fixed by cause (C166-C169); the 17 garment files exit 0 (280 passed, 4 xfailed, /mnt/data/hifipushie/muslin_default/p3/s1.log). | pattern_form.WOVEN / NECK_PIN_W / SHEAR_FREE / SHEAR_RAMP; garment_pull.WOVEN_STYLE | ENFORCED: test_pattern_form (woven fit KATs: test_woven_fit_shears_a_held_sheet_instead_of_stretching, test_neck_point_on_hps, the collar KATs), test_cloth_layers::test_form_fit_under_layer_needs_no_sim, test_garment_pull::test_a_shirt_without_a_tie_is_styled_open_by_default (Joe photo / trace rows on the muslin fit, styled woven) |
| C165 | APPROVED / CURRENT GARMENTS ARE NOT A FIT BASELINE; THE REFERENCES ARE (Joe 2026-10-22, on the muslin default: "checking against the current clothes makes no sense, the current ones are pretty bad"): no garment is held on an old fit (garment_kb fit_holds empty: gc1, dt_tess's coat and unders all on the muslin fit), and "bit-identical to the old result" / "no worse than gc1 / r28h" is never a pass condition. A test that pins an old garment's numbers is either a rule (restate it as the rule) or an old result (rewrite it to the rule or to the reference's numbers); photo / trace / concept gates stay (they are references). Looks: result beside the REFERENCE only, no old-result columns. | garment_kb fit_holds (empty, mechanism kept); tests/test_pattern_form.py test_the_muslin_fit_is_the_default_for_every_garment | ENFORCED: tests/test_pattern_form.py::test_the_muslin_fit_is_the_default_for_every_garment (fit_holds empty; landed on main 2026-10-22) |
| C166 | WHERE NOTHING DRIVES THE CLOTH IT DOES NOT SHEAR; READ "UNSTRETCHED" AS THREADS, SHEAR APART (muslin default 2026-10-22): WOVEN's shear weight (KES G / E, 0.011) takes a shear back 1.1 % a round, so transients outlived the rounds (a free diagonal fold 13 deg / 11.4 % edge stretch; the shoulder-collar KAT's developable bodice 31.8 deg at 120 rounds, 4.0 at 480; sleeves 20 deg on Garrett). pattern_form.SHEAR_FREE on triangles with no driven vertex (touching the form within FREE_REACH, a pin, a layer, a flap's base; attraction is NOT an obstacle) + free flaps keep ARAP cells + SHEAR_RAMP (each relax eases SHEAR_FREE -> SHEAR_STIFF over its first half). Garrett's yoke corner still reads (threads p95 6.0 % vs 1.6 control). Tests that mean "the cloth doesn't stretch" read info grain_stretch_pct + shear_deg <= SHEAR_LOCK_DEG (35, the jamming angle), never principal / edge strain on a woven fit. | pattern_form SHEAR_FREE / FREE_REACH / SHEAR_RAMP / SHEAR_LOCK_DEG; tests test_diagonal_fold_170, test_fold_running_off_mid_length_lies_flat_past_it, test_woven_fit_shears_a_held_sheet_instead_of_stretching, test_two_panel_sleeve_on_real_arm, test_open_front_hangs_without_moving_its_side_seams, test_bridge_hourglass_tube, test_sprung_fall_lies_on_the_shoulders, test_shallow_fall_lands_close_round_the_neck | ENFORCED: the tests named (tests/test_pattern_form.py; landed on main 2026-10-22) |
| C167 | A PIN NEVER DRAGS CLOTH TO A LANDMARK BY STRETCHING IT (muslin default 2026-10-22): the old hard neck pin (200 x an edge) landed a lone panel 129 mm off within 0.5 mm by stretching its threads p99 58 % / max 151 %; the soft pin (NECK_PIN_W 2, as the shoulder) lands a reachable panel within 0.25 mm and leaves an unreachable one short (31 mm) at under half the strain: the miss IS the misfit read. Real garments on the muslin fit: Simon's neck point 3 mm off, Garrett's 12-14. | pattern_form.NECK_PIN_W; test_neck_point_on_hps | ENFORCED: tests/test_pattern_form.py::test_neck_point_on_hps (landed on main 2026-10-22) |
| C168 | ON THE MUSLIN FIT, READ SHAPES BY RULES THE CLOTH OBEYS, NOT BY ONE OLD RESULT; THE UNDER LAYER IS THE FORM FIT AS WORN (muslin default 2026-10-22, Joe: no holds, pass conditions from references / stated rules): (1) an edge's KINK is the turn its own bend doesn't explain (edge_turns kink_deg = turn over 15 mm less half the turn over 30 mm: an arc 0, a crease of c deg c / 2); smooth edges read 3-6.4 deg (the muslin leaf bends down to its point 23 deg per 15 mm, one swoop), planted creases 15 / 20 / 29 deg read 11-15 / 15-20 / 21-27; limit 8 (a 16 deg crease). The old max-turn <= 22 sat between one fit's 20 and q40's 29. (2) tip_read gives threads + shear apart (C166). (3) a layered garment's under layer on the form is the under garment's muslin form fit, styled as worn (pattern_form.under_as_worn) and pressed, passed to cloth.model_body(under=): placed, padded, drafted over it with no sim (on_form refused gc_garrett's jacket 'under_missing' until the shirt was simulated; a pinned sim stood in). (4) OPEN: test 2's right collar tip crumples 0.146 vs closed 0.086 + 0.05: the styling solve is ISOMETRIC on a WOVEN fit; the band-end turn takes the fit's shear / eased compression (tip warp p5 -4.4 %, the roll side -3..-8 %) back as length and buckles; run to convergence BOTH fits crumple (0.27 / 0.28: early stopping hid it on the iso fit). garment_pull.WOVEN_STYLE (the fit's local step in the styling solve) gives tips = closed (0.039 / 0.084) and points 147 apart (concept ~147) but the pull / smooth stops read edge stretch, which counts the shear it allows (4 reds); pattern_form.SHEAR_YIELD (free shear yields once threads strain) styles clean too but opens the points to 156 (5 reds). Both opt-in. | garment_pull.edge_turns kink_deg, tip_read thread_max_pct / shear_max_deg; pattern_form.under_as_worn; cloth.model_body(under=) | ENFORCED: test_garment_pull::test_shirt_collar_hugs_the_neck_at_the_back_and_sides (kink <= 8, planted 20 deg crease fails), test_cloth_layers::test_form_fit_under_layer_needs_no_sim; (4) DIRECTION: the styling stops read as threads, then WOVEN_STYLE on |
| C169 | STYLING STOPS READ THREADS, NOT EDGES; THE STYLING SOLVE IS THE FIT'S CLOTH (muslin default 2026-10-22): "until it would stretch" on a woven means until a THREAD would stretch or the weave would JAM. The styling solve was isometric on a woven fit and gave the fit's compressed tip threads back as a buckle (C168 (4)); the woven styling solve (WOVEN_STYLE) allows bias shear, which lengthens edges without stretching any thread, so the edge-stretch stops misread it (4 reds). Now: every stop (pull, turn tol, smooth, smooth_border; relax / easy-dir reads too) reads thread strain p99 (warp / weft \|len - 1\| of each face's gradient in its thread frame, as tip_read / the fit's grain_stretch_pct) and a PER-PIECE shear p99 lock at pattern_form.SHEAR_LOCK_DEG 35 (a piece already past it: +1 deg; per piece because a pooled p99 hid front.L's free neck cloth at 39.7 deg under 12k collar faces at 20). The styling solve takes the fit's own local step INCLUDING SHEAR_FREE on undriven faces (no held / gripped / pinned vertex; C166): with SHEAR_STIFF everywhere a free sheet trellised (the wobble smooth stalled at 29 of 38.7 deg kink, end-zone wobble kept; points 158 apart vs the concept ~147 +- 7, collar shear p99 22 deg); with the fit's rule the wobble goes 38.7 -> 14.1 (iso 13.2), points 151 / 153.5 apart, collar shear p99 4-9 deg, tips 0.131 / 0.146 (closed 0.086; limits +0.05 / +0.06). Tests that encoded the old read, rewritten: open-by-default "edge p99 <= 7 %" -> threads p99 <= 7 % + every piece's shear under the lock (planted: collar grown 10 % fails); worn-style buttons "tops part > 20 mm with tol 1.0" -> the corner dragged in its own plane jams at 15 mm (shear p99 36.5; isometric 30 mm), stopped by shear / max_move and > 10 mm; sp10 recipe points apart 130-150 (one old result, 137) -> the concept band 140-154 as test 2 (the turned-only collar 66.8 fails). | garment_pull.thread_strain / thread_p99 / thread_faces, _shear_lock, FlatARAP (WOVEN_STYLE default on, shear_free), SHEAR_LOCK_NOISE | ENFORCED: test_garment_pull (all 16: slack strip, smooth, smooth_border wobble, spike reads tests 2 / 3, open-by-default, buttons); suite exit 0 (p3/s1.log) |

The same r29p2 pattern on_form'd with the spec's own worn_open rule (break 64 off CF from the concept, fasten "worn_open"): roll 70 / 58 / 63 L, 68 / 53 R, break 379 / 364 below HPS (concept 386), nothing else pinned. The muslin default's jacket scripts popped worn_open (jon.py / jfit.py), which is why jon1 closed high. Below the break the fronts still hang closed (16 / 12 / 21 vs 71 / 78 / 94): the styling opens them. | pattern_form.on_form (worn_open garments fit open); fit30.py / roll30.py | DOC |
| C170 | STYLING CARRIES THE FIT'S LAYER ORDER AS A FLOOR (garrett jacket fit round 30, 2026-10-23; C157's want): garment_pull.layer_floor(M, X, body, under) -> style(..., floor=): after every solve round (and once after every op: a smooth moves cloth outside the solve) a free vertex nearer a floor than min(gap THICK, its start distance) goes back on it along the floor's normal, only where it stands over it (1.5 x the floor's median edge sideways). Floors: the body and the under garment (cloth slides over them: a vertex not over it at the start gets the gap), each fold's flap over its base (a narrow base across its seam, C119), each stitched patch over its piece (start-only: sewn, doesn't slide; given the gap, the collar fall past the stand's edge was pushed off it, shear 10 -> 20 deg). Evidence: without it Garrett's lapels went 0.50-0.61 through their fronts (C157 reproduced); with the flap floor 0. Open: the UNDER floor stops a jacket opening (the fit lies within EASE of a pressed shirt: threads 7.5 -> 12 % at the relax) -> styled with body + flaps (covered show-through, C153); pocket flaps still sink 9-13 / 25 (a vertex leaves the vertex-normal floor's reach in one move). | garment_pull.layer_floor / FlatARAP._floor / style(floor) | ENFORCED: test_garment_pull::test_layer_floor_keeps_a_pulled_cloth_on_the_body, ::test_layer_floor_keeps_a_patch_on_its_piece |
| C172 | A FIT OVER ANOTHER GARMENT CAN END INSIDE IT: THE UNDER LAYER WAS A SOFT TARGET (garrett jacket fit round 31, 2026-10-23): why the shirt floor "stopped the opening" in round 30. Measured on the worn-open fit (w1h) BEFORE styling: 131 jacket vertices through the pressed shirt (fronts 79 in the top 300 mm, up to 13 mm; backs / stand / collar at the neck) and 442 shirt vertices > 1 mm outside the jacket (yoke at the shoulders, shirt fronts on the upper chest, the spread collar under the lapels): assemble's push off the under layer is a proximal target only (the local step's T) and the lengths win. Styling's shirt floor then fought the solve every round at the rim of that region (one 20-round relax: 45 vertices pushed, up to 175 mm summed per vertex; threads 7.5 -> 12 %). It is not the floor's thickness nor the edge region: the fit is already through. pattern_form.UNDER_FLOOR (the ceiling's mirror: contact weighted LAYER_W in the solve + projected LAYER_GAP out each round) cut it to 18 through / 118 poking, but moved the roll off the concept (150 / 250: 58 / 47 vs 70 / 58, concept 69 / 64), opened a sew gap 4.6 mm, grain max 98 %. Joe: covered show-through is culled anyway (C153): OPT-IN, not pursued. | pattern_form.UNDER_FLOOR (opt-in); underdbg.py / underfloor.py ($FR31) | KAT: test_pattern_form::test_tight_outer_tube_lies_over_the_under_one (on 0 through / grain 20.9 %; off 660 through) |
| C173 | A FLOOR'S REACH IS LOCAL TO THE FLOOR'S OWN MESH (garrett jacket fit round 31, 2026-10-23): why the pocket flaps sank (r30: 9-13 of 25 vertices). The patch floor held a vertex only where it stood over its base: sideways within 1.5 x the base's MEDIAN edge (7.6 mm) of the nearest base vertex. Garrett's front is meshed fine along its lapel rows and coarse under the flap (10-12 mm between vertices): 3-19 of each flap's 20 free vertices had NO floor from the start (level -inf) and sank 6-25 mm. The reach is now 1.5 x the nearest floor vertex's longest edge, capped at 3 x the median (a sliver read 50 mm): every flap vertex has a floor; styled, flaps sunk 1 / 0 of 25 (the 1 is a stitched vertex at -0.0). | garment_pull._local_reach / FLOOR_REACH_CAP / layer_floor / FlatARAP._floor | ENFORCED: test_garment_pull::test_layer_floor_reach_is_local_to_a_coarse_base |
| C174 | STYLING ENDS WITH SMOOTH + PULL DOWN, AS AN ARTIST DRESSES A FIGURE; AN OPEN JACKET OPENS BELOW ITS BREAK ONLY (Joe 2026-10-23 on r30: "it looks like when it styled it open, it made the divots. If I were styling this by hand, I'd smooth it out, pull it down" -- the rope fold at the waist; garrett jacket fit round 31): r30 pulled each front edge break..hem side + out as one border: the break swung 126 mm off CF (fit / concept 64) and the edge curled in at the waist. | C194 | CLOTH BENDING IS READ ON EDGE HINGES, NOT VERTEX MEAN CURVATURE; INTERFACED CLOTH RESTS AT ITS MADE SHAPE, PLAIN CLOTH FLAT (canvas thread, 2026-10-23): the vertex Laplacian term (\|L x\|^2 / A, Bergou) only sees mean curvature, so a strip bent along its length and curled across it costs nothing: a canvassed strip fell as fast as a plain one at 500 x. bending.Bending: per interior edge a 4-point stencil (null on every planar placement, \|e\| x fold angle), weights by interfacing kind (canvas 20, fusible 8, plain 1; entry "kind" or garment key interfacing_kind), rest SIGNED along the hinge normal (unsigned, a roll flips over its stand for free). | bending.py; garment_pull.STYLE_BEND (off); pattern_form.FIT_BEND_KEYED (off); tests/test_bending.py | ENFORCED: tests/test_bending.py (test_hinge_stencil_reads_the_fold_angle, test_canvassed_strip_holds_where_plain_cloth_bends, test_rolled_collar_keeps_its_roll, test_bending_keeps_a_declared_fold) |
| C195 | STIFFNESS NEVER BUYS SHAPE WITH STRETCH: THREADS FIRST, BENDING WITHIN THEM (canvas s2, 2026-10-23; Joe: "it shouldn't really be a choice between stiffness and stretch, that means we're thinking it wrong"): woven cloth barely stretches along its threads, canvassed or not; stiffness changes how it BENDS. In a weighted sum (ARAP + hinges) a stiff piece asked for a shape it can't reach bought it with thread strain (a canvassed strip walked round a bend its threads allow: 12.4 % compression, plain 6.4 %). The order is a hierarchy: with bending on, the styling solve runs bending-free rounds after each round and to convergence at the end, every face's warp / weft held within max(its styling-start length, 1 +/- 1 %) (a Jacobi cap alone can't give back end-to-end strain); the fit caps the interfaced triangles' threads each round. Walked strip: canvas 1.1 % and on the bend's arc (mid -19.3 / -19.4 mm), plain 0.15 %. Like-for-like on the jacket (no holds): the keyed fit did not raise threads (c0_none 11.48 -> c2_none 9.62); the k3 comparison (7.42) had holds supplying the shape. | garment_pull.STYLE_THREAD_FIRST (on with STYLE_BEND); pattern_form.FIT_THREAD_FIRST (on with keyed bending); tests/test_bending.py test_stiffness_never_buys_shape_with_stretch | ENFORCED: tests/test_bending.py::test_stiffness_never_buys_shape_with_stretch |

| C226 | THE THREADS OUTWEIGH THE BENDING WHERE THERE IS BENDING (canvas s15, 2026-10-26): C209's thread weight was one scalar (~13000 on Garrett's jacket) on every face. Within its band a face's threads target is its own current map, so the term is also a lag spring on every move: plain cloth with no bending moved 13000 x slower per hang round and the stiff-styled lower front never hung (length +2 mm in 4000 rounds). Scaling the weight (gravity) up instead makes the canvas droop like plain cloth (strip KAT tip 106 of 108 mm). Each face now weighs MEM x the bending at its own vertices (2 rings dilated, >= 1). Their flat body / shirt colour spans the crop's full height (a 191 px run of flat columns on the upper front, 11 px on the whole front), and grid_gutters counted any full-length flat line unlike the background as a tile border. A contact sheet's gutter is a straight band: its colour stops at the run's edges; a flat region of one picture carries on past the run on many lines (the silhouette widens). A rest is the shape the maker gave the cloth, not the crumples it was found in. A faired rest (high-pass removed) failed: the lapel's crumple hinges are as sharp as its roll's. | garment_pull.BEND_REST_PRESS (GP_BEND_REST_PRESS=1, OPT-IN), press_rest; $S16 rip16.py | ENFORCED: tests/test_bending.py::test_the_canvas_is_pressed_before_it_is_worn |
| C229 | THE FIT'S STRETCH LET GO OVER THE STYLING (canvas s16, 2026-10-26): the threads-first upper bound was max(the fit's length, 1.01), so the stiff styling kept the fit's stretch (stand 33.7 % at the fit). TF_BLEED shrinks the bound's excess linearly to 0 over the styling's ops (no ratchet). On a strip it works (10 % -> 1.05 %); on Garrett's jacket it does not reach the collar (b2L + bleed: collar 15 -> 19 %, stand 18 -> 15): the collar's strain rides on its SHEAR (6 -> 40 deg in the stiff styling's first relax: the PD step's shear weight is SHEAR_STIFF 0.011, so shear is free up to the 35 deg lock). A uniform or per-face SHEAR_FREE in the PD step took the collar to 5 % but crumpled the forepart (rough 2.4-3.1 mm) and swung the lower fronts out (hem sweep 510-524): rejected, reverted. A bound that shrinks moves strain to where the shape still needs it. | garment_pull.TF_BLEED (GP_TF_BLEED=1, OPT-IN, in no candidate) | ENFORCED: tests/test_bending.py::test_the_fits_stretch_is_let_go_over_the_styling |
| C230 | THE LAST WORD TAKES NO LENGTH OUT OF THE SHEAR (canvas s17, 2026-10-26): the collar's 6 -> 42 deg shear in the stiff styling's first relax is the threads-first LAST WORD (STYLE_THREAD_FINAL bending-free rounds until no face is past its bound), not the bending (bending weight x 0.002: still 42; no floors / no under layer / no folded-hinge rule: 41-42; the 20 bending rounds alone: 9). Under STYLE_TF_PD the last word's only shear resistance is the SHEAR_STIFF 0.011 mix up to the 35 deg lock, so faces past their bound were brought back by shearing neighbours (a rhombus keeps its sides). FINAL_SHEAR_HOLD: the last word's lock per face = max(its shear entering the last word, its styling start's): collar 22.3 % / 41.7 deg -> 10.0 / 8.4, stand 17.5 / 22.5 -> 18.6 / 14.8 in that relax. A projection that may only restore lengths must not trade them for another freedom. | garment_pull.FINAL_SHEAR_HOLD (GP_FINAL_SHEAR_HOLD) | ENFORCED: tests/test_bending.py::test_the_last_word_takes_no_length_out_of_the_shear |

| C233 | CLIENTS DON'T REFRESH TOOL LISTS MID-SESSION (mcp core, 2026-10-26): a usability trial agent called enable_toolset("cloth,human") and never saw the tools (hosts ignore tools/list_changed), and 100 schemas cluttered every session. A small native core + find_tool + call_tool reaches every tool in any host: the server registers only the core (guide, find_tool, call_tool, models, look, check, measure, history, revert, review_view, kit_reference); find_tool ranks every tool by word match (name, description, domain, guide section; idf) with parameters + an example call; call_tool validates args against the tool's signature (unknown / missing / wrong type -> an error naming what it expects) and returns its result as a native call would (images too). enable_toolset, HIFIPUSHIE_TOOLSETS, HIFIPUSHIE_CALL_TOOL and the listChanged advertising are gone; TOOL_GROUPS is grouping metadata only; next: lines name call_tool("<tool>", {...}) | server.py find_tool / call_tool / _register_core, TOOL_GROUPS; INSTRUCTIONS; tools_guide.md | tests/test_find_tool.py (garment query top 3, empty query lists all, call_tool runs a non-core tool + images, bad args, every tool reachable), tests/test_toolsets.py |
| C234 | A HANG TEAR CAN BE A CONTACT FAULT, NOT THE LOAD PATH (hang round 1, 2026-10-26): the old settle (384 %) and the new tethered hang (337 %) tore the hoodie sleeve at the same forearm edges; the let-go had left 11 sleeve vertices > 10 mm inside the arm and hard nearest-side contact pushed them out the wrong side. Before blaming the solver, count deep penetrations at the phase start (diag2.py); hang_tethered skips contact deeper than HANG_DEEP. Also: stretch_pct is abs (compression counts): read the signed extension before calling a fit "stretched". | pattern_form.HANG_DEEP, docs/state/hang.md, /mnt/data/hifipushie/hang/diag2.py (landed 2026-11-02 from archive/worktree-agent-ae2f8e92379fe5450; hang_tethered / HANG_TETHER not on main) |
| C235 | THREADS ARE HARD IN EVERY FIT; A MISFIT SHOWS AS WHAT THE FIT CAN'T REACH (Joe 2026-10-26: "I don't understand why it CAN stretch, why are we allowing it?"; "we had a whole spike about this and apparently we never learned, or it's gated behind optins"): the muslin fit (C161-C167) made warp / weft STIFF, a weighted term in one solve with the seams, the seat's attraction, the body's push and the pins, so stronger pulls still stretched them (Garrett's jacket threads max 124 %, hoodie 26 %), the hard cap (C195) ran only on interfaced cloth, and fit_garment gated on FACE strain (counts the bias's shear: hoodie 13.9 % with threads at 6.6 %), which C64 / C166 had ruled out. Now: every round ends with every triangle's threads projected within the cloth's cap (2 % woven; a knit piece FIT_THREAD_CAP + what its own seams ask: a rib band drafted at 85 % gets 17.6 %, the body 2 %), the woven shear locked at SHEAR_LOCK_DEG (clamped threads alone moved the jacket's misfit into shear, p99 33 -> 41 deg), and each relax phase ends with threads + seams + closures converged (Gauss-Seidel by colour; the old Jacobi mean stalled at 7 % after 2000 sweeps). fit_garment gates on threads PAST their cap (p99 <= 1 %) + shear, and lists what the fit couldn't reach as pattern faults by piece (cloth inside the body, open seams, neck points short: neck_point_mm listed only w >= 100 pins, so the soft neck pin's miss was never reported). | pattern_form.THREADS_HARD (PF_THREADS_HARD), piece_stretch_asked, _thread_limit (colour GS + lock), _hard_pass, face_threads, info inside_by_piece; next_steps.THREAD_OVER_P99_MAX / misfit_lines; server fit_garment | ENFORCED: tests/test_pattern_form.py::test_threads_are_hard_in_the_fit, ::test_thread_limit_locks_a_jammed_shear, ::test_a_knit_piece_stretches_as_far_as_its_seams_ask REBUILT 2026-10-26 (92b29237): a clamp after a soft solve undid folds / contact (3 tries); now the solve predicts and one Gauss-Seidel loop projects thread STRETCH + the phase's seams + contact planes together (contact last: a misfit is threads past cap by piece, never cloth through, C172); shortening stays soft (capped both ways the loop cycled). KATs whose answer needed stretch (plumb hold 14 %, sprung fall 4.8 %) are restated, not loosened. |
| C236 | A CREW NECK IS NOT A SHIRT NECKLINE; A RIB RESTS AT ITS SEWN LENGTH; THE STRAIN MAP READS THREADS (T-shirt trial t3, 2026-10-27: the band read as a mock-turtle; the diag map painted bias shear while the gate read threads): (1) the block drafted every neckline as a shirt's (half the neck girth + ease, sized for a collar): the tee's was a high jewel neck hugging the neck. | C259 | STIFF PARTS KEEP THEIR OWN SHAPE; THEY ARE NOT FITTED TIGHT TO THE BODY (Joe 2026-10-29, collar modelled from pattern chains: 'stiff parts aren't fit so tightly to the body, they have to keep their own shape'). Interfaced / stiff pieces (collar, band, cuffs, plackets, lapels) hold their pattern lengths exactly and bend little: they rest on the high points of what is under them and bridge the hollows. A rule that lays a stiff edge at a fixed offset from the body traces every bump (the collar's edge waved over the shoulder / trapezius). Applied as: pattern lengths hard; stiff edges with a strong bending penalty; contact as "can't sink in" + a light rest pull, never "follow the surface". Also: lines across a piece can bend but not stretch (chord <= flat length), a point's corner triangle is rigid. PROVISIONAL (outline spike /mnt/data/hifipushie/folds_ab/D/outline.py, closed collar O13; not yet in the library). |
| C260 | FOLDED STIFF PIECES ARE BUILT FROM THE PATTERN'S OUTLINE, NOT SOLVED AS CLOTH (Joe 2026-10-29, collar: "the best it's ever looked ... we've finally nailed the shape"). Each edge / fold line is a chain of the pattern's link lengths (hard); only bends are free; the pattern's lines across the piece hold the chains together (exact on the interfaced band and where the fall lies flat, may-shorten where the leaves curve; point triangles rigid). Stiff edges stiff all along; contact = can't sink in + light rest. Open state = the stiff band's own shape unbuttoned (pressed curve round the back, straight past the side neck), started from a swung-open guess; the collar is solved on the held band; soft fronts hang from it. Surfaces = smooth fill between the fixed chains. Look at the piece alone first, judge against the reference for the idea of the style. PROVISIONAL (spikes/collar_outline; not yet in the library; dimples on the fold open). |

| C277 | THE HANG IS A MINIMUM, NOT A HOLD (Joe 2026-10-28: 'why is it doing per-model fixes? Shouldn't we be building general solutions?'). RULE: hanging cloth rests at the minimum of gravity + bending under hard threads (no stretch past the cap; a woven's own-weight strain ~0.2 % = 0, a knit's its cap) and contact with Coulomb friction at its supports (mu 0.4 / 0.3, textile on skin / textile); bending from the fabric's Peirce bending length (KB bending_length_mm; c^3 = B/w, K = L'A^-1 L in metres: no mesh size). The old hang's bending length was c^3 = FIT_BEND Abar / (FIT_STIFF HANG_G) = 25-35 mm, set by mesh density; its stiff rest lengths + anchors stop the cloth turning. LANDED OPT-IN (pattern_form.HANG_QS False, hang_qs: dynamic relaxation, implicit bending, Gauss-Seidel hard pass, pins released by Coulomb, global taut solve; bending_length(g); on_form passes bend_len). MEASURED (towel KAT, $H4/towel.py): converged (300 sweeps a step) it PASSES with no tuned number (spread 0.1 / 1.1, hems +0.4, p99 1.5, inside 0) at 725 s; at a garment's budget (10-30 sweeps) Gauss-Seidel leaves it 2 % long / ratcheted short at the edges; a global penalty solve of the taut threads holds their directions and stalls the turn. NOT DEFAULT; garments not run. OPEN: a convergent fast inextensibility solve (docs/state/hang.md NEXT). (landed 2026-11-02 from archive/worktree-agent-a08b87c766601e235; was C246 on its branch; hang_qs not on main) |

Anchored barycentrically on the flat mesh, re-made on any positions of it (flat, built, hung): details never enter the sim, they are carried; meshes separate from the cloth (details.pkl). Step 7 done only when every inventoried detail is placed with step 1's count; until then chain_presim writes 7 todo and 7a waits. Open: `stiffens` is declared, not yet felt by the build; layering clearance (T9). |

Now the landmark card draws the model's landmarks with their words, with every fit refusal; each landmark is checked against where its neighbours put it with the model's proportions (worst out first; by mm; kept only with a why); a width sits at its landmark's row or AT a garment height (a hem: the body's section at that height). | garment_fit, garment_fit_marks, garment_fit_look |

| C357 | A TAPE DOWN THE BODY KEEPS ITS LINE (s3-fix3, Joe on s3-2's look: "why does 18 have a kink in it?"), for any body. Now the point is taken the same way and put on the tape's own line, in tailor.measure's down() (every FreeSewing back / front length) and body_tape's down_to alike. |
| C358 | FREESEWING GETS ITS OWN WAIST AND HIPS (Joe 2026-11-04, after s3-3: "Waist level + FS hips"). Garrett's tape waist (70% pelvis -> chest joint) sat 23 mm over his iliac crest, so FreeSewing's waistToHips was 23.4 mm and Simon's back dart (0.75 of it) 17 mm long. FreeSewing's waist is the narrowest section (its docs: where you fold bending sideways): now taken when the body clearly dips near the skeletal estimate (>= 1.5 % under both ends of -5..+8 cm; Tess +50 mm, Garrett +15, Simon +5), else the skeletal point (a belly with no dip, cloth2_heavy, keeps it). FreeSewing's hips: its standard sizes' waist-to-hips (130 mm men / 125 women: chosen, not surveyed) scaled by stature (0.073), below that waist (fsHips / waistToFsHips; Garrett 129 mm, 901 girth); the true crest (ANSUR II, 4-9 cm under the waist on our bodies) stays highHip. Every waist-based length moves with the waist (Garrett hpsToWaistBack 499 -> 479). |
| C359 | A TILTED GIRTH ROUND A LIMB'S ROOT (Joe 2026-11-04, after s3-4: "Add tilted girth"), for any body. Now {kind: girth, through: [A, B], around: limb} takes the loop in the plane through both landmarks facing along the limb, its convex hull (tape_tilted.py); where that plane first cuts into the torso (Garrett: a 1518 mm loop round both) the lower landmark steps down 1 cm at a time to 4 cm, said in the answer; still none (a heavy body, cloth2_heavy) is refused by name. Armholes: Garrett 405 mm (the arcs 403), Simon 419, Tess 373, dg_mh 451: 0.42-0.44 of the chest, both sides equal. The body's arm root has no ease: a jacket's armhole (~43-46 cm) adds it at the draft. |

(1) A measurement read or set in the design: s4-1 dropped hpsToBust from jaeger's list and wrote it into the measurements as hpsToWaistBack - 226; now the runner reports measurements a part reads that aren't there (FreeSewing's own warning) and any the design sets, and both are refused: retake step 3 with the name. (3) darts [{piece, legs}]: a dart open on the outline, its legs sewn to each other (the seam check holds their lengths; they must meet at the tip); sewn in the mesh.
