(1) draft_pattern refuses an option outside its design's range, saying a pct option is a FRACTION (4 % = 0.04; s4-2 gave 4: a cap 3x the size); pattern_parts says so beside the options. (2) pattern_parts marks parts a package doesn't export, and the exported part to reach each through (jaeger's front / back: frontFacing.from). (3) A .R piece with no edges, its .L declared: the refusal points to pair: true (edges declared once). (4) A 0 mm or NaN edge refused by name (s4-2: "cannot convert float NaN to integer", a back neck with two coincident points); the converter's NaN refusal says where to look. (5) Step 3's answer lists FreeSewing's blocks it can draft and what each other needs (every jacket block needs hpsToBust, which s3-4 hadn't taken: s4-2 retook step 3). |

Also: grain as a word refused with what it takes (degrees, 90 = lengthwise); a closure's "at" pairs are point names. |

Now a declaration whose pair step 1 names by side (X.L + X.R) is expanded first (draft_pairs.expand) and the expansion validated; a step 1 naming the piece once (X, cut as a pair) keeps the old path. |
| C365 | A NOTCH, A DRAFT ORDER AND A CHAINED CAP, SAID (trial s4-4 suit jacket, flat gate PASS). s4-4 found by trying that the notch check reads FreeSewing notch SNIPPETS, not outline points; that a part made `from:` another doesn't inherit its `after:` (FreeSewing), which broke the draft order; and that a two-piece sleeve's cap, whose seams don't land on the body's, is sewn as ONE chained seam into the whole armhole. The guide says all three; pattern_check's notches-off line says what a notch is; an unsewn step-1 seam's refusal names the chain way. |
| C366 | PAIRS FINISHED, AND A LAPEL IS NOT A COLLAR (trial s4-5 suit jacket, flat gate PASS). C364 validated a pair declared once but s4-5 found the rest half done: (1) a seam naming one side of a pair by step 1's own name (front.L:shoulder = back.L:shoulder) now brings its .R twin (".L brings .R"); darts and marks on a pair's bare name are expanded per side; (2) the flat gate reads the expanded declaration, and the cut row counts a copy under its own name or its base ("not cut: front.L ... not in step 1: front.L"); (3) a .L mark with mirror: true marks its .R detail too, any kind (placement already mirrored follows / rows); (4) a roll fold on a front or facing is its lapel, not a collar's fall; a piece named exactly a role ("front") has that role. Tried and reverted: the fall check on the collar's whole neck edge - on a notched collar the edge sewn to "neck" runs up the gorge, so it failed good collars (s4-4 0.64, s4-5 0.75); it needs the neck / gorge split declared. s4-3..s4-5 still PASS. |
| C370 | A SMOOTH MASS IS ONE PRIMITIVE, NOT BLENDED BLOBS (Baynard Racing, Puddles: head and body as one pear came out a snowman, then a capsule grooved at every overlap; a rounded box bands where its planes meet its edges). Now `"shape": "loft"` (sections joined by C2 splines, superellipse caps) and `"shape": "super"` (superellipsoid, squareness 2 = ellipsoid to box); fit moves sections, caps and squareness, fit(init_loft) measures sections off the outlines and keeps a row another part crosses. Proof: pud_loft vs pud_blobs under raking light, blind review names bands only on the blobs. A fit step that makes an element invalid is rejected, not raised. |
| C371 | JUDGE A FACE SHAPE IN A LOOK, NOT AN EXPORT; CARRY A REST FACE, DON'T BAKE IT (face-pose, Puddles). Every face-shape check cost a 4-5 min export + GLB re-import. look(shapes=) runs the export's own shape code on the export-fine mesh (cached, ~10 s; body median 0.36 mm from the GLB). face_shapes.rest goes in glTF mesh.weights: baking measured 20-57 mm off for a closed mouth / viseme (clamped negative weights). | facepose.py, look(shapes=), face_shapes.rest |
| C372 | A FOLDED EAR IS ONE SHEET ON A CURVE, NOT HINGED OR CHAINED BLADES (Baynard Racing, Puddles: `blade` only bends a few cm; two blades hinged at an edge read as two plates; a chain of short blades along a Bezier came out knuckled at every joint). Now `"shape": "sheet"` (sheet.py): a spine (natural spline or cubic Bezier), stations of half width / half thickness / cup / twist (C2 splines in arc length), a rotation-minimising width axis, edges rounded by the thickness; refused when the spine bends tighter than the sheet is thick. fit moves spine points ("pos") and station widths ("size"). tests/test_sheet.py. |
| C373 | A STUB LIMB IS SIZED BY ITS GIRTH, AND BODY SKIN ROUND IT IS THE HIPS' (Baynard Racing, Puddles seated, legs in the body). The hip's arc zone was sized by the thigh's length (5.5 cm, thinner than it is round: 2 cm reach) and missed the skin; the distance blend carried thigh weight 13 cm up the belly and seated it tore into a web. `rig.limb_girth` + ARC_GIRTH / ARC_ROOTS / ARC_STUB_UNIT / ARC_BODY in `_arc_bend`: a limb root (hip, shoulder) whose girth > 0.25 x length is a stub, its zone unit girth / 0.1, and skin past 1.1-2.5 x girth from its axis gives its whole chain's weight to the parent. People's limbs bit-identical (test). Seated > 3x stretched edges 2442 -> 60. tests/test_rig_stub.py |
| C374 | A STUB'S SEATED CORRECTIVE TURNS NOTHING ROUND THE PIVOT; IT KEEPS THE BODY (Baynard Racing, Puddles seated, legs in the body). correctives.UpLeg_flex90 exists only on a stub (keys(girth=rig.limb_girth): the frames the weights used). Its target (`_stub_target`): LBS, then on the INNER side the hip family's move sharpened by the limb's share (STUB_SHARP: belly skin with a weight tail stays put; seated the stub's thigh goes into the body) and on the OUTER side parent-side skin held at the turned thigh's underside (STUB_FLOOR x girth). Dead ends: turning by the share round the pivot (skin 2x girth behind swung 10 cm down), ARAP of the ramp (a flap), sharpening the back too (the weights put the bottom on the leg: a sheet), counting the knee in the family, delta mush (ridges). A floor from the thigh's own skin came out on the front (a pig's thigh has no back skin): use the girth. | correctives.py `_stub_target`, tests/test_rig_stub.py |
| C375 | A CREATURE'S LIMB LOOPS START WHERE THE LIMB LEAVES THE BODY, AND ARE CUT ON THE FINE MESH (Baynard Racing, Puddles seated). Without a template (parts.<p>.topology "limbs", limbloops.py) a limb is a tube of quad rings shot onto the exact surface, crease rings packed at hinges; its root ring is the highest cross-section it owns (rays < 1.35 x radius): Puddles' hip AND knee sit inside the belly, the anatomy's hip plane cuts the belly, so the ring goes where the leg leaves it (z 0.10). Junction rings walk outward from the axis (walking up the limb runs into the belly's underside), are evened by arc length (Laplacian shrinks them back onto the leg), and the body is cut and zipped on the fine mesh before decimating with the rings as border (cut after, the one-triangle crotch strip let both holes merge). Seated, same triangles: hips > 2x 48 -> 17 edges; the fold follows the rings; crotch notch unchanged (weights). | limbloops.CLEAR / _even / build; tests/test_limbloops.py |
